Topological slow entropy, sequence entropy, and generalized [ T , T − 1 ] \delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779 systems Thanks: Address. Faculty of Mathematics and Computer Science, Jagiellonian University, Kraków, Poland. Thanks: Email. nicanor.vargas@uj.edu.pl
Nicanor Carrasco-Vargas

Abstract
. We consider topological dynamical systems given by skew products S ⋊ UNKNOWN T \mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012 , where S : Y → Y \mathchar 29011\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29017 is a subshift, τ : Y → ℤ \mathchar 28956\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29018} is a continuous cocycle, and T \mathchar 29012 is an arbitrary invertible topological system. For fixed ( Y , S , τ ) \delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28956\delimiter 84054785 it may happen that all systems of the form S ⋊ UNKNOWN T \mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012 have the same topological entropy, and thus it arises the problem of distinguishing two such systems. We show that if T 1 \mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28721} and T 2 \mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28722} are invertible topological dynamical systems with different topological entropy then S ⋊ UNKNOWN T 1 \mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28721} and S ⋊ UNKNOWN T 1 \mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28721} can be distinguished using slow entropy as introduced by Katok and Thouvenot. We prove a similar result under the assumption that the fiber systems have different slow entropy at some scale (this can be applied if T 1 \mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28721} and T 2 \mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28722} have both zero entropy, or have the same entropy). These results require rather mild assumptions on ( Y , S , τ ) \delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28956\delimiter 84054785 , and can be applied to some entropy-zero systems in the base. We generalize classical results in the theory of sequence entropy, which were proved by Goodman with the additional assumption of finite topological dimension. We show that the measure-theoretic sequence entropy of any system is bounded by its topological sequence entropy. Under an extra assumption on the sequence we establish a variational principle, and prove that the topological sequence entropy of a system equals its topological entropy multiplied by a positive constant that depends only on the sequence.

37B40, 37A35, 37B02

Theorem 1.4

It is natural to ask if the scales that we construct can be used for measure-theoretic
\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
systems. This is not the case in general, in the sense that the scale may not capture the entropy of the fiber. For instance, consider a skew product
\mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012
, where
\mathchar 29011
is the shift over
\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}
,
\mathchar 28956
is given by
\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29049\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785
for all
\mathchar 29049
, and
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785
is an arbitrary invertible system. Endow
\mathchar 29017\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29016
with the product
\mathchar 28951\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28950
of the uniform measure
\mathchar 28951
on
\{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}
, and a
\mathchar 29012
invariant Borel probability measure
\mathchar 28950
on
\mathchar 29016
with finite entropy. It is not difficult to verify that the measure-theoretic slow entropy of
\mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012
with the measure
\mathchar 28951\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28950
and the scale
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84054785
from

is zero. This means that the correct estimate for Hamming (pseudo)-balls associated to
\mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012
grows much slower than the one for Bowen balls. This is a different situation from

for measure-preserving systems will be studied in future work.

Another invariant considered in this work is sequence entropy. This invariant is obtained by replacing in the definition of entropy the iterates
\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891\dots
by
\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 24891\dots
, for an arbitrary sequence of natural numbers
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721}
. The invariant obtained is denoted
\mathchar 29032^{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785
for a topological system
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785
, and
\mathchar 29032^{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785
for a measure preserving system
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 28994}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28950\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785
. This invariant was first introduced for measure preserving systems in
[
32
]
, and was later extended to topological systems in
[
15
]
. Subsequent works in this subject include
[
14
,
6
,
18
]
.

In this work we strengthen a classical result in the theory of sequence entropy. Namely, the next result was proved in the seminal work of Goodman
[
15
, Theorems 4.1 and 4.4]
under the extra assumption of finite topological dimension.

Theorem 1.6
.

Let
\mathchar 29016
be a compact metric space and let

(2)

Remark 1
.

Part (1) in
Theorem

1.6
was proved by Goodman
[
15
, Theorem 3.1]
, by Eberlein
[
12
, Theorem 4.3]
, and more recently by Huang and Ye
[
19
, Theorem 2.6]
. These proofs rely on the relation
\mathchar 29032^{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29032^{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785
, stated in Kushnirenko’s work
[
32
]
. This relation was disproved in
[
33
]
(see also
[
23
, Section 4]
). To our knowledge the proof presented here is the first to avoid this issue. See also
Remark

2
.

There are families of systems for which both sequence entropy and slow entropy have shown to be useful invariants, see for instance
[
25
]
. This may give the impression that both invariants have similar distinguishing power. In
Example

5.5
we find a family of systems where sequence entropy provides no information, but slow entropy does.

2.
Preliminaries

In this section we review definitions and basic facts about sequence entropy and topological slow entropy. The reader is referred to the survey
[
23
]
for more details.

The lower topological slow entropy

is defined similarly, replacing

One easily verifies that

2.5.

A subshift on

3.
Sequence entropy and proof of

In this section we prove

1.6
. It will be convenient to fix for the rest of the section the objects with which we will work. Fix a compact metric space
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016
, a Borel
\mathchar 29012
-invariant probability measure
\mathchar 28950
on
\mathchar 29016

\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}}

Definition 3.1
.

For
\mathchar 28998\Subset\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}
,
\mathchar 28943\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
, and
\mathchar 28942\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
, let

be the minimal number of Bowen
\mathchar 28943\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28998
-balls needed to cover a subset of
\mathchar 29016
with measure
\mathchar 28950
at least
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28942
.

A. Katok proved
[
27
, Theorem 1.1]
that if
\mathchar 28950
is ergodic and
\mathchar 29012
is invertible, then the measure-theoretic entropy
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785
can be computed as

\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\lim_{\mathchar 28943\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28720^{\mathchar 8235}}\left\delimiter 67273472\limsup_{\mathchar 29038}{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 29038}}\log\operatorname{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24891\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28943\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28942\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785

for any fixed
\mathchar 28942\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
. This formula fails without assuming ergodicity. For instance, if
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 29016
has a subset with measure
\mathchar 28942\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
where
\mathchar 29012
acts as the identity function, then the formula at the right side will be
\mathchar 28720
and in particular different from
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785

In the next result we show that without the ergodicity assumption, if we take the limit
\mathchar 28942\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28720
in the formula above, then the inequality
\mathchar 12820
is still true. This holds in the more general context of sequence entropy.

Proposition 3.2

Let
\mathchar 28950
be a
\mathchar 29012

\mathchar 29016

Then the measure-theoretic sequence entropy

Proposition 3.3

Remark 2
.

Goodman’s proof of part (1) in
Theorem

1.6
is similar in ideas to the well-known proof of the variational principle of Misiurewicz
[
34
]
, see also
[
35
,
37
]
. That is, by approximating a measurable partition by open (or closed) covers one first establishes a relation of the form
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28995
, for some constant
\mathchar 28995\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
. Then one replaces
\mathchar 29012
by its powers
\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 29038}
or products
\mathchar 29012\mathchar 8706\dots\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29012
to remove the constant
\mathchar 28995
. The second step is not possible for sequence entropy, which may not behave like entropy with respect to powers and products
[
33
,
20
]
. We also note that the argument using Hamming (pseudo)-balls can be generalized to prove that measure-theoretic entropy is at most topological entropy in the context of amenable group actions (compare with
[
30
, Theorem 9.48]
).

Remark 3
.

In the next section we will prove estimates for
\operatorname{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28998\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28943\delimiter 84054785
for an invertible transformation
\mathchar 29012
and for a set
\mathchar 28998\Subset\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
(
Proposition

4.2
). These can be interpreted as a finitary version of part (3) in
Theorem

1.6
. Indeed, an alternative proof of part (3) in
Theorem

1.6
can be obtained by taking
\mathchar 28998\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 29038}\}
in
Proposition

4.2
, item (2).

4.
Proof of
Theorem

1.3
and
Theorem

1.4

In this section we prove our results regarding topological slow entropy of skew products,
Theorem

1.3
and
Theorem

1.4
. We recall that notation and terminology for Bowen metrics and subshifts are reviewed in
Section

2
.

4.1.
Estimating
\operatorname{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28998\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28943\delimiter 84054785

In this subsection we present some preliminary computations that will be needed for the proof of
Theorem

1.3
.

Let
\mathchar 29012
be a continuous and invertible transformation of the compact metric space
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785
. Here we prove estimates for
\operatorname{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28998\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28943\delimiter 84054785
, where
\mathchar 28998\Subset\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
is not necessarily an interval, but its “gaps” are bounded by a constant

.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}
. A set
\mathchar 28998\Subset\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
is said to have
\mathchar 29037
-

if the set
\mathchar 28998\mathchar 8235\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\}
is an interval. We define
\mathchar 28995_{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28998\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}
by
\mathchar 28995_{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28998\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28998\mathchar 8235\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\}\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28998\delimiter 69640972
.

The main result of this subsection is the following.

.

{{\mathchar 28721\over\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28998\delimiter 69640972}}\log\operatorname{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28998\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28942\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28995_{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28998\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28943\delimiter 84054785

One easily derives an analogous statement in terms of open covers using

Proposition 4.3

(Theorem 6.8 in
[

]
)
.

The topological entropy
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\sup_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29013}}\inf_{\mathchar 28998\Subset\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}{{\mathchar 28721\over\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28998\delimiter 69640972}}\log\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29006\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29013}^{\mathchar 28998}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Here

ranges over finite open covers for
\mathchar 29016
.

Proof of
Proposition

4.2
.

. We start by proving the statement in the case

This is possible as
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\lim_{\mathchar 28942\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28720^{\mathchar 8235}}\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28942\delimiter 84054785
, also recall that we defined

in

\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28720}
such that for all

4.2.
Sketch of the proof of

1.3

Here we present an informal outline of the proof of

.

\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29049\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785
,
\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29017
. We also fix an arbitrary invertible topological system

\mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29011\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
.

Theorem

1.3
states that there is a scale
\{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84054785\}_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720}
depending only on

Remark 5
.

If
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785
is a
\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}
-subshift instead of a
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
-subshift then all the arguments presented here can be easily adapted. One only needs to redefine
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\delimiter 84054785
as
\mathchar 29004_{\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\delimiter 84054785
instead of
\mathchar 29004_{\{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\delimiter 84054785
(as in
Section

2.5
).

5.
Examples

Given a tuple
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28956\delimiter 84054785
of a subshift
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29017
and a continuous cocycle
\mathchar 28956\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
, we consider the family of skew products
\mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012
, where
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785
is an arbitrary invertible topological dynamical system. In the next result we prove a sufficient condition on
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28956\delimiter 84054785
that implies that all systems of the form
\mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012
have the same topological entropy. This justifies the application of other invariants different from topological entropy.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785
be a subshift, and let
\mathchar 28956\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
be a continuous function such that for all
\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29017
we have

\lim_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12833\mathchar 561}{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891

The nontrivial inequality is
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29011\rtimes_{\mathchar 28956}\mathchar 29012\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785

be arbitrary. The hypothesis ensures that there is
\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28720}
such that for all
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28720}
we have
{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12604\mathchar 28945\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722
for all
\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29017

one obtains that for all
\mathchar 29038
large enough

Here

We remark that the condition in the last result is met when

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 28721

by definition of

and
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29035}
. Thus
\mathchar 29042_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049^{\mathchar 8707}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29006
for all
\mathchar 29038
. This implies that
\mathchar 29038\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049^{\mathchar 8707}\delimiter 84054785
is bounded, a contradiction to the hypothesis.
∎

Recall that a continuous cocycle
\mathchar 28956\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
is called a coboundary when
\mathchar 28956\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29031\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29031\mathchar 8718\mathchar 29011
for some continuous function
\mathchar 29031\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}

[

states that if
\mathchar 28956
is not a cocycle then
\mathchar 29038\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785
is unbounded for every
\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29017
. From this an the previous result we obtain the following.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785
be a minimal subshift and let
\mathchar 28956\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29017\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
be a continuous map which is not a coboundary. Then
\mathchar 28956
is
\mathchar 28721
-unbounded.

This result provides a large class of systems where

1.5
can be applied. We now review one specific example in detail.

Example 5.4

Proposition 5.6

Let

Proof sketch.

Let
\mathchar 28950
be the uniform measure on
\{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}

\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}

\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}
the proportion of words

, which converges to
\mathchar 28721
as
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12833\mathchar 561
by the previous observation.
∎

In contrast to the measure-theoretic case, it is easy to observe
2
2

2

In this case one easily derives from

that

.
that with this system in the base, the topological entropy of

Proposition 5.8
.

The topological slow entropy of a continuous flow on a compact metric space equals the topological slow entropy of its time one map, for any scale.

Proof.

\mathchar 29012\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29016\mathchar 8706\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016

.

\mathchar 28943^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12349\min\{\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28943\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\}

. It suffices to take an arbitrary pair of elements

with

, and prove

. We take an arbitrary element
